# Proposed OEIS comments: A268777 to A276293

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A268777

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268777>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2517](../papers/02501-03000/02517-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + 10*a(n-2) - 32*a(n-3) - 47*a(n-4) + 40*a(n-5) + 38*a(n-6) - 12*a(n-7) - 9*a(n-8).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(13 + 13*x - 74*x^2 - 36*x^3 + 66*x^4 + 26*x^5 - 21*x^6 - 9*x^7) / (1 - 2*x - 7*x^2 + 2*x^3 + 3*x^4)^2. - _Colin Barker_, Jan 15 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 38 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

What the entry records as unsettled:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. ```

## A268788

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268788>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10671](../papers/10501-11000/10671-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

## A268892

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268892>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10672](../papers/10501-11000/10672-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269001>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10673](../papers/10501-11000/10673-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A269006

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269006>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2925](../papers/02501-03000/02925-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 10*a(n-1) - 31*a(n-2) + 24*a(n-3) + 21*a(n-4) - 18*a(n-5) - 9*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(1 - x)*(1 - 3*x)*(1 - 2*x + 3*x^2) / (1 - 5*x + 3*x^2 + 3*x^3)^2. - _Colin Barker_, Jan 18 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 54 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026

## A269088

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269088>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10674](../papers/10501-11000/10674-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A270086

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A270086>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3832](../papers/03501-04000/03832-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 11 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 24 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A270257

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A270257>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [1149](../papers/01001-01500/01149-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) -2*a(n-3) +a(n-4)
> Empirical for n mod 2 = 0: a(n) = 3*n^2
> Empirical for n mod 2 = 1: a(n) = 3*n^2 - 3

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true, and the closed form behind it is exact for every n. The shape of the triangle is fixed and it is the alphabet 0..n that grows, so this is not a walk count and no transfer matrix applies. Sum over the sets of adjacent pairs instead: a set of pairs forces the values along each of its connected components to alternate, so a bipartite component has n+1 choices (n for the target n+1) and a component with an odd cycle forces 2x = t, which is possible only for one parity of n. Inclusion-exclusion then gives a(n) = A(n) + (-1)^n C(n) with A and C explicit polynomials, and the recurrence above is exactly (E-1)^(deg A+1) (E+1)^(deg C+1) applied to that, so it holds for every n. The two n mod 2 formulas on this entry are the same statement read at each parity, and are exact as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A270934

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A270934>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3833](../papers/03501-04000/03833-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 26 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 24 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A273019

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A273019>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [11273](../papers/11001-11500/11273-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +(241*n+56) *(2*n+1) *(n+1)*a(n) +(482*n^3-25561*n^2+13831*n-56) *a(n-1) +(-48682*n^3+225897*n^2-300131*n+125310) *a(n-2) +9*(n-2) *(2651*n-3183) *(2*n-3) *a(n-3)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, May 16 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A273351

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A273351>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10947](../papers/10501-11000/10947-PROOF.pdf), [11041](../papers/11001-11500/11041-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(13*n-40)*a(n) +(-55*n^2+211*n-129)*a(n-1) +(38*n^2-212*n+255)*a(n-2) +(-6*n^2+56*n-75)*a(n-3) +(13*n^2-66*n+3)*a(n-4) -(3*n-13)*(n-6)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 06 2016
> Conjecture: n*(n-3)*(n-2)^2*a(n) -(n-3)*(2*n-3)*(2*n^2-6*n+3) *a(n-1) +(2*n^4-16*n^3+41*n^2-36*n+5) *a(n-2) +2*(n-1)*(2*n-5) *a(n-3) +(n-2)*(n-5)*(n-1)^2 *a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 06 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A274723

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A274723>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3026](../papers/03001-03500/03026-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 4*a(n-2) + a(n-3) - 3*a(n-4) - 4*a(n-5) for n>7.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(5 + 9*x - 24*x^2 - 45*x^3 - 21*x^4 - 4*x^5 + 8*x^6) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 4*x)*(1 + x + x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 30 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 155 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
